decisions:
  - key: "other — lexical_in_project (new in task 27), resolve_sibling and check_corpus_links' lexical_join each walk path components with the same Normal/ParentDir/CurDir arms. The three differ where it matters: resolve_sibling and lexical_in_project refuse a rooted path and test containment against different bases, lexical_join drops a root component and tests nothing, so a shared helper would have to take both policies. — `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifacts.rs:2587`"
    outcome: waived
    reason: The three walkers take different policies for a rooted path and for the containment base, so one helper would carry two flags to save about ten lines each; the divergence is the point of each.
    decided-at: 2026-11-15T22:47:15Z
    decided-by: rule
  - key: "bug — with an outward link in the feature directory, a done spec reads clean: true with a non-empty skipped (family outward-link, reason target-unparseable) for a scenario carrying unresolved open questions, a review.md with blocking: true, a tasks.md with unchecked boxes or a spec.md, where AC3 makes the spec's own unreadable artifact a blocking finding at done because an unreadable scenario with unresolved questions would otherwise pass the gate built to catch it. The tests a_scenario_linked_outside_the_project_is_skipped_while_the_run_continues and a_spec_linked_outside_the_project_ends_the_run_with_the_skip_alone pin the skip, and the plan records the choice, so this is a design tension between AC3 and the sibling-symlink-trust-boundary scenario rather than an unnoticed gap. check-review-gate reads scenario questions through collect_scenario_open_questions with no guard, so the completion gate does not depend on this result. — `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifacts.rs:298`"
    outcome: waived
    reason: "The plan and the sibling-symlink-trust-boundary scenario decide that a link out is recorded target-unparseable and never opened, with clean: true plus a non-empty skipped meaning partially examined (QUAL-CLAIM-001), and artifact-unreadable names a file the run tried and failed to read, not one it declines to open; the pre-done gate reads the scenario questions itself."
    decided-at: 2026-11-15T22:47:15Z
    decided-by: rule
  - key: "other — 101 of the 116 references Family 39 counts as not judgeable by construction (run on this tree at HEAD: 544 files, 2400 references, exit 0) name a bare basename that exists elsewhere in the tracked tree but not at the citing directory or the project root, for example ductus-procedure.md section Pre-run Migrations in framework/migrations/ductus-rename.md line 16, ductus.md section Derived values in framework/reference/analyze.md line 329, spec.md and data-model.md in many scenarios. The primitive resolves a document only from the citing file's directory and then the project root (stated in its module docs and scenario anchor-reference-kinds), so these are excluded as 'absent' though the document exists; a renamed section behind such a pointer still fails no gate, contrary to the commit's aim that a renamed section fail CI instead of going unseen. The unexamined count is reported on stderr and recorded under anchor-target-unjudgeable, so nothing reads as passed. — `runtime/src/primitives/resolve_anchor.rs:568-644`"
    outcome: waived
    reason: The resolution bases (citing directory, then project root) are the decided design of scenarios anchor-reference-kinds and shared-constitution-references-are-judged, and the excluded references are counted and recorded unexamined, not reported clean. Resolving a bare basename to a unique tracked file would be a new requirement for the owner to decide (a basename can match several files), not a defect of this change.
    decided-at: 2026-11-15T22:47:15Z
    decided-by: rule
  - key: other — check-rule-ids tries the retired-rules arm before the shipped-index arm, so a rule a spec lists that the framework still ships but the project never installed (FE-XSS-001 with only a backend file loaded, run on a fixture) is reported retired, not not-installed, and step 5 then records no rule-not-installed unexamined target for it — `runtime/src/primitives/check_rule_ids.rs:91-114`
    outcome: waived
    reason: The scenario passes a listed ID that no loaded rule file defines, which is what the arm does, and a spec lists only a rule it records the retirement of; an author listing a rule the framework still ships has made an authoring error the list's owner can read from the retired state, and changing the precedence is a behavior decision for the scenario, not a mechanical fix.
    decided-at: 2026-11-15T22:47:15Z
    decided-by: rule
  - key: "other — Family 40's finding for a due rule tells the remover to re-point or classify each citing file and does not name retired-rules: as the place a record of the retirement is classified, though scenario a-record-cites-a-removed-rule says the remover records it there — `scripts/audit/deprecation-removal.sh:262-263`"
    outcome: waived
    reason: The message is the one 026's scenario family-40-deprecated-rules-are-removed-on-time states and test-deprecation-removal.sh asserts, and it names the classification the remover makes; the observation concerns the pipeline's own audit machinery, not the code under review breaking its contract.
    decided-at: 2026-11-15T22:47:15Z
    decided-by: rule
  - key: perf — section_label_lines counts the newlines before the heading (first = content[..heading_start].matches newline count) on every call, a cost proportional to the file prefix per rule heading, and check-rule-ids uses only whether the result is empty, so it pays for the line index it never reads (about 100 headings over the 121 KB security-backend file, a few megabytes of scanning per run) — `runtime/src/primitives/rule_sections.rs:214-218`
    outcome: waived
    reason: Measured by reading, the added scan is a few megabytes of newline counting per check-rule-ids run over the largest rule file, far below the cost of the file reads around it, and one shared reader is what keeps check-rule-ids and read-deprecation-labels from disagreeing.
    decided-at: 2026-11-15T22:47:15Z
    decided-by: rule
  - key: convention — the test the_primitive_labels_what_check_rule_ids_calls_deprecated is named for agreement between the primitive and check-rule-ids but asserts only the primitive's own output for a blockquoted label, never calling check-rule-ids; the agreement holds by construction (one shared section_label_lines), and the commit message records that the primitive's tests fail when the shared reader's label token is mutated — `runtime/src/primitives/read_deprecation_labels.rs:328-345`
    outcome: waived
    reason: "QUAL-TEST-001's first Not-flagged clause covers it: the commit message records the mutation (the shared reader's label token) that turns the primitive's tests red, and the property the name claims is held by one shared function rather than by two copies a test could compare."
    decided-at: 2026-11-15T22:47:15Z
    decided-by: rule
  - key: "other — framework/reference/analyze.md is 62,697 bytes against the 50,000-byte read size: 72fd2ce6 cut it to 49,777, the next two commits (0a7787b4 of 041, 12ea0e44 of 063) left it at 49,917 and 50,479, and later commits including this group's f3ce20be (+1,596), a54ef591 (+1,017), 0cc0cc1e (+220), 8f3b4407 (+147) and a42867ae (+18) took it to 62,697; 079's ticked AC18 says it fits one read at that spec's close and no longer holds, and nothing (scripts/lint-governing-size.sh holds only AGENTS.md and the constitution) enforces it — `framework/reference/analyze.md`"
    outcome: waived
    reason: The claim belongs to 079's AC18 and 067's contract states none about the reference's size; the reference is read through read-section by heading, which the command's Instructions require, so a host never reads it whole, and the framework has no size gate for a reference file to hold it to, so this is an observation about the pipeline's own machinery
    decided-at: 2026-11-15T22:47:15Z
    decided-by: rule
  - key: other — an unreadable plan.md on a done spec yields three blocking findings for one file, from link-adjacent-drift, body-path-existence and plan-consistency (reproduced on a fixture), so blocking-findings counts one defect three times — `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifacts.rs:932-958`
    outcome: waived
    reason: Each family records an artifact it could not read through record_unreadable_artifact by design (scenario unreadable-scenario-is-reported), so a family's silence never reads as examined, and the findings are one defect named by three families; collapsing them would make a family's result depend on another's
    decided-at: 2026-11-15T22:47:15Z
    decided-by: rule
  - key: "other — lists_a_file counts any word holding a slash as a listed file, so a placeholder such as N/A or tbd/none under Affected Files passes plan-consistency (reproduced: the section reading N/A or tbd/none raised no finding) — `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifacts.rs:609`"
    outcome: waived
    reason: "The plan decision and 8f3b4407 state the rule on purpose: the section is a planning aid the template calls non-exhaustive, so the blocking check catches only a section left empty or holding its scaffold and never judges a spelling or its content"
    decided-at: 2026-11-15T22:47:15Z
    decided-by: rule
  - key: convention — the plan template's Affected Files comment calls the section a planning aid that need not be exhaustive, but plan-consistency now blocks a plan whose section lists nothing outside comments, and the template says nothing of that block; the constitution's template-rule alignment names template and check as one change, and f3ce20be touched no template — `framework/templates/spec/plan.md`
    outcome: waived
    reason: The scaffold fails the two content checks on purpose, as 8f3b4407's scaffold test requires, while the template's Implements link passes the references-its-spec check by default; an author fills the section at /plan, whose readiness gate and step 8's finding name what is missing, so the alignment the constitution asks for is carried by the check's own message
    decided-at: 2026-11-15T22:47:15Z
    decided-by: rule

    outcome: waived

  - key: other — the adopter-corpus-link-integrity scenario's first Behavior bullet says resolution is lexical and never canonicalized, so the result does not depend on symlinks; judged against the new link check it still holds (the target is joined textually and never canonicalized, the base is resolved only to compare, and a link out is refused, which keeps the answer independent of what lies outside the checkout), but the Behavior and Edge Cases say nothing of a target that exists beyond the base being reported broken or of a file under a link out being skipped; the decision is stated in 067's data model, the compiled description and the changelog, as task 33 asked — `specs/067-analyze-and-audit-checks/scenarios/adopter-corpus-link-integrity.md:21-25`

  - key: perf — leaves_project_by_link resolves the project root through canonical_base once per candidate path, where check-corpus-links' Containment resolves its base once per scan; each call is a handful of lstat and readlink calls on the root's components — `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifacts.rs:2610`
    outcome: waived
    reason: the cost is a few system calls per backticked path in one feature's criteria and body, no result changes, and hoisting the base is a signature change through three call sites, not a mechanical edit.
    decided-at: 2026-11-15T22:47:15Z
    decided-by: rule
  - key: other — Containment::judge counts an unresolvable base (canonical None) as leaving, so every link in the scan would be reported broken with guidance saying the target leaves the project, when the cause is the base; the failure is loud and the base is the project root the run already opened — `runtime/src/primitives/check_corpus_links.rs:574-580`
    outcome: waived
    reason: fail-closed and visible (every link is a finding, exit non-zero), the base is a directory the process is running in so the state needs the root removed mid-run, and a distinct guidance is a new state, not a mechanical edit.

# Review — 067-analyze-and-audit-checks

## Summary

067's review of its reopening. The window runs from `c91391ad`, the reopen's base, to `a2de525e`: 58 commits of 067's tasks 14 and 17 to 35 and its review round `2be18da6`. Six agents ran all five passes over six groups of those commits, each reading the nine loaded backend and cross rule files whole and 067's spec, plan, data model and tasks whole. By their coverage ledgers they read 74 of the 182 scope entries whole, every scenario among them. Of the rest, 31 entries and the CHANGELOG were read at their window hunks (each group its own commits'), 14 generated copies were checked against their sources, 54 were not opened, since another review record holds a read of their current content, and 8 are the files only this run's fix round changed, which its re-read reads. 18 findings, all fixed in the run but three: BE-INPUT-004 on check-review-gate's cross-spec read is deferred to 066 task 23 (owner decision), which releases it; QUAL-CLAIM-001 on an unparseable migrations registry is waived on the owner's 2026-10-26 decision (`2be18da6`); and BE-INPUT-004 on derive-references' load_registry is no violation, since a registered service's checkout lies outside the project by contract (specs 030 and 040, `validate_no_traversal`'s config-sourced boundary, which now names derive-references). The fix contains every path 067's primitives draw from committed text (inventoried primitive by primitive, check-unfolded-specs and check-departures found by the sweep), states the class once in 022's plan, bounds five results, closes the seven contract breaks (check-artifacts' placeholder, plan-reference and list-marker tests in code; four scenario and data model claims corrected by their delivering commits), and fixes the chores; read-spec lists no scenarios directory its caller covers. The rule waived the other observations, each with its reason. The re-reads added: four stale doc and test comments fixed, analyze step 3 and the bootstrap's Pre-run Migrations step 8 read their results' file form, and traverse-deps refuses an absolute dependency name, which a join put in place of the spec root. Round 1's re-read was split between two agents by path, its results merged unchanged.

3 fix round(s): 3 re-read, 0 diff-checked, 0 exempt, 0 not re-read.

## MUST violations (blocking)

*None.*

## SHOULD violations (advisory)

*None.*

## Low-confidence findings

*None.*

## Waived findings

### WAIVED: QUAL-CLAIM-001 — a framework/migrations.toml that does not parse reads as watermark attribution, the same result as an absent one

- **File**: `runtime/src/primitives/check_orphaned_references.rs:505-520`
- **Rule**: A result that reports a clean, empty, or in-sync state SHOULD distinguish *examined the subject and found nothing* from *could not examine the subject*. When a code path skips part of its subject, cannot reach it, or has no basis to inspect it, its output SHOULD say so — a distinct return variant, an accompanying status or guidance field, or a message naming what was not examined — not a bare zero, empty collection, or success string.
- **Finding**: read_registry returns None for a missing registry and for one that does not parse, and run turns None into attribution watermark with no skipped entry. 2be18da6 changed the doc comment to say so instead of recording a skip (the owner's 2026-10-26 round fixed the doc), and the compiled description now states that the result does not distinguish the two (checked in ductus check-orphaned-references --help). So the condition is documented and chosen, not removed: the output itself still cannot tell a ductus checkout whose registry is broken from an adopter's. Low confidence for that reason; the parent may waive it on the recorded decision.
- **Auto-fixable**: no
- **Waived**: an unparseable framework/migrations.toml reading as watermark attribution is the decided behavior: 067's review round 1 (2be18da6, 2026-10-26) documented it rather than add a skipped entry, and the compiled description states that the two cases read alike; the owner kept that choice on 2026-11-15

## Deferred findings

### DEFERRED: BE-INPUT-004 — check-artifacts' cross-spec-impact-drift opens another feature's spec.md and scenarios through a link that leaves the project

- **File**: `runtime/src/primitives/check_review_gate.rs:1119-1171`
- **Rule**: User-supplied values MUST NOT be used directly in filesystem paths. Filesystem operations MUST resolve the canonical path and verify it falls within the expected base directory before opening the file.
- **Finding**: check_cross_spec_impact_drift (check_artifacts.rs 1126-1159) calls cross_spec_impact_states, whose classify_cross_spec_entry screens the slug but then reads specs/<other>/spec.md and each file in specs/<other>/scenarios/ with read_to_string (links_back). OutwardLinks::find walks only the checked feature's directory, so a link in another feature's directory is never found and never covers anything. Reproduced with the built binary on a done spec declaring cross-spec-impact: [043-other]: with specs/043-other/spec.md a symlink to a file outside the project that carries the back-link, check-artifacts reports no cross-spec-impact-drift finding; with the link pointing at an outside file lacking it, the finding appears. The result therefore depends on the content of a file outside the project, which task 29's commit title says check-artifacts never reads (the plan and data model scope the walk to the checked feature's directory, so this read sits outside what they describe). The fix lands partly in 066's check-review-gate code, which shares classify_cross_spec_entry.
- **Auto-fixable**: no
- **Deferred**: to `specs/066-audit-records-and-the-completion-gate/tasks.md` task 23

- perf — section_label_lines counts the newlines before the heading (first = content[..heading_start].matches newline count) on every call, a cost proportional to the file prefix per rule heading, and check-rule-ids uses only whether the result is empty, so it pays for the line index it never reads (about 100 headings over the 121 KB security-backend file, a few megabytes of scanning per run) — `runtime/src/primitives/rule_sections.rs:214-218` — **waived**: Measured by reading, the added scan is a few megabytes of newline counting per check-rule-ids run over the largest rule file, far below the cost of the file reads around it, and one shared reader is what keeps check-rule-ids and read-deprecation-labels from disagreeing. (decided by the rule)
- convention — the test the_primitive_labels_what_check_rule_ids_calls_deprecated is named for agreement between the primitive and check-rule-ids but asserts only the primitive's own output for a blockquoted label, never calling check-rule-ids; the agreement holds by construction (one shared section_label_lines), and the commit message records that the primitive's tests fail when the shared reader's label token is mutated — `runtime/src/primitives/read_deprecation_labels.rs:328-345` — **waived**: QUAL-TEST-001's first Not-flagged clause covers it: the commit message records the mutation (the shared reader's label token) that turns the primitive's tests red, and the property the name claims is held by one shared function rather than by two copies a test could compare. (decided by the rule)
- other — a_scenario_a_spec_run_cannot_read_is_a_hard_fail returns before asserting when the process can still open a mode-0 file (running as root), so a root-run suite passes with the unreadable-scenario hard fail removed, and nothing records that the skip ran — `runtime/src/primitives/validate_frontmatter.rs:955-986` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- convention — no test pins the JSON of check-rule-ids' retired state: the unit tests assert the struct the primitive returns, and the schema round-trip test builds a result with retired empty, so a serde attribute change that drops the retired flag or list from the JSON the host reads leaves the suite green (the omitted-when-empty half is held by the analyze-basic golden) — `runtime/src/schema/primitives.rs:9120-9137` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- bug — is_placeholder_criterion calls any criterion that starts with < and ends with >, or starts with { and ends with }, a placeholder, so a real criterion such as <b>bold</b> output is escaped by the <i> or {a: 1} parses into {b} is a blocking spec-integrity finding (reproduced with the release binary on a fixture, two findings); 067's plan decision says text wholly inside <> or {}, and the reference says wholly in — `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifacts.rs:495-506` (breaks the spec's contract) — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- bug — plan_references_spec derives its number with feature.split('-').next(), which for a branch-scoped directory whose identifier holds a hyphen (my-branch.1-slug) yields my, so a plan that merely contains the word my passes the references-its-spec check (reproduced: a plan reading Implements the thing for my users. raised no plan-consistency finding); AGENTS.md says a feature directory is not necessarily NNN-slug and parse_feature_dir/FeatureForm decide, and the plan decision names the feature directory or its number — `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifacts.rs:617-629` (breaks the spec's contract) — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- bug — lists_a_file recognises a list item only by the prefixes '- ', '* ' and digits then '. ', so '+ Makefile' and '1) Makefile', which CommonMark reads as list items, are not counted and a plan listing only an extensionless root file that way gets a blocking plan-consistency finding (reproduced); the plan decision and 8f3b4407's message say a list item with text lists a file in any spelling — `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifacts.rs:596-605` (breaks the spec's contract) — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- convention — an_empty_or_placeholder_criterion_is_blocking, the test that names the placeholder behavior, exercises only the empty text, TBD, todo, n/a and the two bracket forms, so removing the tbc, fixme, placeholder, ellipsis arms of is_placeholder_criterion, or widening the bracket test (the defect in the first observation), fails no test, though the reference and the data model list all seven words and the ellipsis (QUAL-TEST-001: a test should fail when the behavior it names is removed); f3ce20be's message records eight hand mutations that no mutation list under scripts/mutation holds — `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifacts.rs:6294-6303` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — task 31 (0cc0cc1e) changes a result the plan classifies as breaking (a done spec whose plan.md the check passed over now carries a blocking finding) but runtime/CHANGELOG.md has no entry for it; the Unreleased section's rule is that obligations are written as their changes land, and f3ce20be's entry for the two families does not mention an unreadable plan — `runtime/CHANGELOG.md:155-162` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — spec.md names the check-artifacts families in two enumerations that task 23 and spec 079 left stale: the Artifact checks paragraph omits spec integrity, plan consistency and cross-spec impact drift, and AC1's list omits cross-spec-impact-drift, which the module doc, the data model and the reference all list as a family — `specs/067-analyze-and-audit-checks/spec.md:24` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — plan.md's BE-COMPAT-001 table classifies each interface change but has no row for task 28 (a54ef591), which added the exec reason decision-reasons-not-checked to the unexamined-by-reason set that 066's class table and AnalyzeBlock document as closed; the table's own lead sentence says every interface change is classified, and task 23's row covers integrity-not-checked — `specs/067-analyze-and-audit-checks/plan.md:90` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — plan.md says 13 done specs predate the plan template's link and name the spec only in the title; re-counted on 2026-11-15 as plans with no markdown link to their own spec.md, 13 plans match but 10 of those specs are done and 3 are in-progress (003, 007, 012), so the count states the wrong population — `specs/067-analyze-and-audit-checks/plan.md:67` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — docs/analyze.md's What it checks table has a row for each analyze family including Grounding but none for task 28's Decision reasons check (step 15, advisory, with its own exec reason), so a reader of the user-facing doc cannot learn analyze reads recorded decisions; a docs commit must name 067 in its subject — `docs/analyze.md:40` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — the AnalyzeBlock doc comment on unexamined_by_reason, which f3ce20be and a54ef591 each extended with a new reason, lists the closed reason set in its two classes but omits anchor-target-unjudgeable (excluded by construction) and anchor-target-unreadable (could not be read), both of which the reference's Unexamined targets section lists and resolve-anchor emits since 8651d466 (group 3's commit); an enumeration is a claim of its own — `runtime/src/schema/primitives.rs:571-600` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — framework/reference/analyze.md is 62,697 bytes against the 50,000-byte read size: 72fd2ce6 cut it to 49,777, the next two commits (0a7787b4 of 041, 12ea0e44 of 063) left it at 49,917 and 50,479, and later commits including this group's f3ce20be (+1,596), a54ef591 (+1,017), 0cc0cc1e (+220), 8f3b4407 (+147) and a42867ae (+18) took it to 62,697; 079's ticked AC18 says it fits one read at that spec's close and no longer holds, and nothing (scripts/lint-governing-size.sh holds only AGENTS.md and the constitution) enforces it — `framework/reference/analyze.md` — **waived**: The claim belongs to 079's AC18 and 067's contract states none about the reference's size; the reference is read through read-section by heading, which the command's Instructions require, so a host never reads it whole, and the framework has no size gate for a reference file to hold it to, so this is an observation about the pipeline's own machinery (decided by the rule)
- other — an unreadable plan.md on a done spec yields three blocking findings for one file, from link-adjacent-drift, body-path-existence and plan-consistency (reproduced on a fixture), so blocking-findings counts one defect three times — `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifacts.rs:932-958` — **waived**: Each family records an artifact it could not read through record_unreadable_artifact by design (scenario unreadable-scenario-is-reported), so a family's silence never reads as examined, and the findings are one defect named by three families; collapsing them would make a family's result depend on another's (decided by the rule)
- other — lists_a_file counts any word holding a slash as a listed file, so a placeholder such as N/A or tbd/none under Affected Files passes plan-consistency (reproduced: the section reading N/A or tbd/none raised no finding) — `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifacts.rs:609` — **waived**: The plan decision and 8f3b4407 state the rule on purpose: the section is a planning aid the template calls non-exhaustive, so the blocking check catches only a section left empty or holding its scaffold and never judges a spelling or its content (decided by the rule)
- convention — the plan template's Affected Files comment calls the section a planning aid that need not be exhaustive, but plan-consistency now blocks a plan whose section lists nothing outside comments, and the template says nothing of that block; the constitution's template-rule alignment names template and check as one change, and f3ce20be touched no template — `framework/templates/spec/plan.md` — **waived**: The scaffold fails the two content checks on purpose, as 8f3b4407's scaffold test requires, while the template's Implements link passes the references-its-spec check by default; an author fills the section at /plan, whose readiness gate and step 8's finding name what is missing, so the alignment the constitution asks for is carried by the check's own message (decided by the rule)
- bug — check-corpus-links' stderr line for a skipped file reads unreadable, but an outward-link skip is a refusal, not an unreadable file, and the line names no remedy; the shipped pre-commit hook blocks every commit on such a skip (the CLI gates on skipped-count above zero), so an adopter whose spec root holds a symbolic link out of the repository reads a misleading word on a blocked commit — `runtime/src/main.rs:532` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — framework/reference/fold.md step 14's rationale says check-corpus-links reads through a symbolic link; since 7f40a52e a link that resolves outside the repository's work tree is skipped outward-link and never read, so the sentence no longer holds unqualified (a behavior change needs a prose-claim sweep; task 33's owed sweep named only the plan decision and the skipped-targets scenario row) — `framework/reference/fold.md:33` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — bounded_lists.rs's module comment says a list file's key is, besides the three it names, what spec 080's data model gives, but resolve-anchor (the scanned path's file stem) and read-deprecation-labels (the segment paths) take their keys from 067's data model alone, and 080's key sentence names neither — `runtime/src/primitives/bounded_lists.rs:17-21` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — path_key, extracted from read-section by 7f40a52e and now shared by resolve-anchor, is the filter that keeps a user-supplied path's stem out of the list file's directory name (the first line of defense for BE-INPUT-004, with write()'s validate_contained behind it), and no test names its document fallback: removing the character filter, or the empty-stem case, fails no test (the one mutation touching it, M7, replaces the whole call with a constant) — `runtime/src/primitives/bounded_lists.rs:50` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — no test drives an exec analyze walk through resolve-anchor past the read size: the walker restores lists in take_result before the tally (read at runtime/src/interpreter/mod.rs lines 238-275, correct), and sibling walks have such a test for check-artifact-size and check-departures, but resolve-anchor's unresolved advisory count in the file form is held only by the restore_lists unit test, so a tally that read the result before the restore would under-count unresolved anchors as clean with every test green for this primitive — `runtime/src/interpreter/mod.rs:2053` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — 045's data-model row for outside-project still says a checked path's lexical resolution leaves the project; since 7f40a52e a path through a symbolic link that resolves outside it is recorded the same way — `specs/045-decision-state-drift-detection/data-model.md:88` — **waived**: the row states the lexical half, which still holds, and links 067's check-artifacts-skipped-targets scenario, whose outside-project row (updated by 7f40a52e) states both halves; the closed set's wording is 067's data model's, which names the link case. The fix would land in 045's directory. (decided by the rule)
- other — 051's AC15 annotation says the dogfooded hook and the frontmatter lint are held by nothing and that 067's task 34 adds the check; Family 41 now holds both, so the annotation describes the prior state — `specs/051-branch-scoped-spec-numbering/spec.md:121` — **waived**: the annotation is 051's record of the tree on 2026-11-14 and names task 34 as the owed check; 051's task 36 owns its wording before 051's analysis, and the fix would land in 051's directory. (decided by the rule)
- other — the adopter-corpus-link-integrity scenario's first Behavior bullet says resolution is lexical and never canonicalized, so the result does not depend on symlinks; judged against the new link check it still holds (the target is joined textually and never canonicalized, the base is resolved only to compare, and a link out is refused, which keeps the answer independent of what lies outside the checkout), but the Behavior and Edge Cases say nothing of a target that exists beyond the base being reported broken or of a file under a link out being skipped; the decision is stated in 067's data model, the compiled description and the changelog, as task 33 asked — `specs/067-analyze-and-audit-checks/scenarios/adopter-corpus-link-integrity.md:21-25` — **waived**: task 33 names the data model and the two descriptions as the places that state the decision, and all three do; the scenario's requirements (report every relative link that resolves to nothing, lexically, with spans stripped and exclusions counted) still hold for every target inside the base. (decided by the rule)
- other — task 35 (and task 33's second bullet at line 29) cite 026's analysis.md as recording the owner's 2026-11-15 deferral, but 026's committed analysis.md at HEAD (last commit 25e4234e, 2026-11-13) records deferred: 0 and names neither 067 nor either finding — `specs/067-analyze-and-audit-checks/tasks.md:47` — **waived**: the authority for each deferral is the owner's decision, which the task commits b63f0cf3 and a15b6cf6 record; the record the sentence cites is 026's own analysis run to write. (decided by the rule)
- bug — in Families 39 and 40, a list file holding valid JSON that is not an object (an array) makes lists.get raise AttributeError, which the except clause (KeyError, TypeError, OSError, ValueError) does not catch, so the python exits with a traceback instead of the precondition finding the file's other bad shapes get; read, not driven (a Bash fixture command was denied): the traceback leaves no counts line, so Family 39 reports its 'examined none' finding and Family 40's failed render is a finding, so no clean pass results, but the message is the wrong one — `scripts/audit/shipped-anchor-resolution.sh:180` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — compute, bound and Labels in read_deprecation_labels.rs, and bound in resolve_anchor.rs, are pub but called only within their own module and its tests (run is the one entry); the check_corpus_links shape they copy is pub because main.rs calls its bound — `runtime/src/primitives/read_deprecation_labels.rs:44` — **waived**: each is called, so none is dead code, and run's doc comment links to them, which a private item would turn into a private intra-doc link; the visibility mirrors the sibling primitives' compute/bound split and widens nothing a caller can misuse. (decided by the rule)
- perf — leaves_project_by_link resolves the project root through canonical_base once per candidate path, where check-corpus-links' Containment resolves its base once per scan; each call is a handful of lstat and readlink calls on the root's components — `runtime/src/primitives/check_artifacts.rs:2610` — **waived**: the cost is a few system calls per backticked path in one feature's criteria and body, no result changes, and hoisting the base is a signature change through three call sites, not a mechanical edit. (decided by the rule)
- other — ProjectRepository::work_tree_root pops one component of the spelled project path for each component of the canonical prefix, so it names the work tree's root only when the spelled project path ends in the same components as its canonical form; every runtime entry takes the project path from current_dir(), which is physical, so the assumption holds today, but nothing states that a project path spelled through a symbolic link in its last components would give a wrong containment base — `runtime/src/primitives/mod.rs:2504` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
- other — Containment::judge counts an unresolvable base (canonical None) as leaving, so every link in the scan would be reported broken with guidance saying the target leaves the project, when the cause is the base; the failure is loud and the base is the project root the run already opened — `runtime/src/primitives/check_corpus_links.rs:574-580` — **waived**: fail-closed and visible (every link is a finding, exit non-zero), the base is a directory the process is running in so the state needs the root removed mid-run, and a distinct guidance is a new state, not a mechanical edit. (decided by the rule)
- other — collect_markdown stats each entry with is_dir(), which follows a link out of the base, before containment.leaves screens it, so a non-markdown entry that is a link out is stat'd through the link and an outward directory link is stat'd before it is skipped; only the file type is learned and nothing is read or listed (the spec root itself is the finding) — `runtime/src/primitives/check_corpus_links.rs:336` — **fixed** (decided by the rule)
